Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe… I mean, I still like being with my husband and cooking with the family. I don’t want to say there’s no pleasure at all. It’s just most days it feels kind of muted, like that gray film I mentioned. I can be there and go through the motions, but I’m not really feeling the enjoyment the way I used to… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d call it anxious, exactly. I mean, I worry about normal things, like work or family stuff, but I haven’t really noticed it being the main thing lately. It’s more just… I’m not sure. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your symptoms were significantly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, it’s kind of been fairly steady, I guess. Some days are definitely heavier than others, but I can’t think of a clear period where it was dramatically worse. Maybe I’m not remembering it the right way, but nothing really stands out like that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are your symptoms usually present for most of the day, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: It’s… usually there most of the day, I think. I can still do things, like be with my husband or cook with the family, so it’s not like I’m completely shut down. But that gray feeling is kind of hanging over things most days, and even when something should feel nice, I’m more just going through the motions. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the gray film over everything and not really enjoying things?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s been a while. Maybe a little over three years now—about three years and a few months. It seemed to start around when I got moved into that higher-responsibility role at work, mentoring adjusters and helping oversee the new workflow systems. I don’t want to make it sound like I couldn’t handle it, because I did handle it, but that’s about when this gray feeling started hanging around. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just a few weeks, where your symptoms were noticeably worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I could clearly point to. There are weeks that feel harder, sure, but I don’t know that I’d say they were noticeably worse than now for a few weeks straight. It’s more like it stays at this same dull level most of the time, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms ever intensified to the point where you felt significantly worse than you do currently?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ve had some rougher days here and there, but I don’t think it’s ever gotten dramatically worse than it is now. It’s more like it’s been steady, if that makes sense. I hope I’m answering that the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms ever been worse at some point in the past than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure I can say it was worse before. It’s been pretty steady, I think. Some days or weeks feel heavier than others, but not like a clearly worse period that stands out. I hope that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I can still do things and be there with my family, but most days it’s like there’s this gray film over everything. Even when my husband is being kind, or we’re cooking together with the family, I can go through the motions… I just don’t really feel the enjoyment the way I used to, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have, somewhat. I don’t mean I never enjoy anything at all, but most days it’s like there’s a gray film over everything. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but probably about three years or so—maybe three years and a few months. It started around when I was moved into that higher-responsibility role, mentoring adjusters and helping oversee the new workflow systems. I kept thinking I’d adjust to it, you know?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I can still do things and be there with my family, but most days it’s like there’s this gray film over everything. Even when my husband is being kind, or we’re cooking together with the family, I can go through the motions… I just don’t really feel the enjoyment the way I used to, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have, somewhat. I don’t mean I never enjoy anything at all, but most days it’s like there’s a gray film over everything. Even when my husband is being sweet, or we’re cooking with the family, I can do it and smile and all that… I just don’t really feel it the way I used to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe… I mean, I still like being with my husband and cooking with the family. I don’t want to say there’s no pleasure at all. It’s just most days it feels kind of muted, like that gray film I mentioned. I can be there and go through the motions, but I’m not really feeling the enjoyment the way I used to… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d call it anxious, exactly. I mean, I worry about normal things, like work or family stuff, but I haven’t really noticed it being the main thing lately. It’s more just… I’m not sure. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your symptoms were significantly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, it’s kind of been fairly steady, I guess. Some days are definitely heavier than others, but I can’t think of a clear period where it was dramatically worse. Maybe I’m not remembering it the right way, but nothing really stands out like that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are your symptoms usually present for most of the day, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: It’s… usually there most of the day, I think. I can still do things, like be with my husband or cook with the family, so it’s not like I’m completely shut down. But that gray feeling is kind of hanging over things most days, and even when something should feel nice, I’m more just going through the motions. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the gray film over everything and not really enjoying things?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s been a while. Maybe a little over three years now—about three years and a few months. It seemed to start around when I got moved into that higher-responsibility role at work, mentoring adjusters and helping oversee the new workflow systems. I don’t want to make it sound like I couldn’t handle it, because I did handle it, but that’s about when this gray feeling started hanging around. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just a few weeks, where your symptoms were noticeably worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I could clearly point to. There are weeks that feel harder, sure, but I don’t know that I’d say they were noticeably worse than now for a few weeks straight. It’s more like it stays at this same dull level most of the time, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms ever intensified to the point where you felt significantly worse than you do currently?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ve had some rougher days here and there, but I don’t think it’s ever gotten dramatically worse than it is now. It’s more like it’s been steady, if that makes sense. I hope I’m answering that the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms ever been worse at some point in the past than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure I can say it was worse before. It’s been pretty steady, I think. Some days or weeks feel heavier than others, but not like a clearly worse period that stands out. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time when your symptoms seemed to worsen all at once?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not all at once, anyway. It’s more like it just kind of… sits there, if that makes sense. I’ve had days that feel harder, but I can’t think of a time where it suddenly got much worse. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms changed in intensity over the past three years?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been mostly the same level, not really big swings. It’s been going on for about three years… maybe three years and a few months now, since I got moved into that higher-responsibility role where I was mentoring adjusters and helping oversee the new workflow systems. It kind of started around then and has just stayed there, if that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Did your symptoms ever get noticeably worse for at least two weeks at any point in the past three years?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, there have definitely been stretches where I felt more worn down, but I can’t honestly point to a two-week period where it clearly got noticeably worse. It’s been more consistent than that, if that’s okay to say.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

I can still do things, like be with my husband or cook with the family, so it’s not like I’m completely shut down. But that gray feeling is kind of hanging over things most days, and even when something should feel nice, I’m more just going through the motions. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the gray film over everything and not really enjoying things?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s been a while. Maybe a little over three years now—about three years and a few months. It seemed to start around when I got moved into that higher-responsibility role at work, mentoring adjusters and helping oversee the new workflow systems. I don’t want to make it sound like I couldn’t handle it, because I did handle it, but that’s about when this gray feeling started hanging around. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just a few weeks, where your symptoms were noticeably worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I could clearly point to. There are weeks that feel harder, sure, but I don’t know that I’d say they were noticeably worse than now for a few weeks straight. It’s more like it stays at this same dull level most of the time, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms ever intensified to the point where you felt significantly worse than you do currently?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ve had some rougher days here and there, but I don’t think it’s ever gotten dramatically worse than it is now. It’s more like it’s been steady, if that makes sense. I hope I’m answering that the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms ever been worse at some point in the past than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure I can say it was worse before. It’s been pretty steady, I think. Some days or weeks feel heavier than others, but not like a clearly worse period that stands out. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time when your symptoms seemed to worsen all at once?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not all at once, anyway. It’s more like it just kind of… sits there, if that makes sense. I’ve had days that feel harder, but I can’t think of a time where it suddenly got much worse. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms changed in intensity over the past three years?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been mostly the same level, not really big swings. It’s been going on for about three years… maybe three years and a few months now, since I got moved into that higher-responsibility role where I was mentoring adjusters and helping oversee the new workflow systems. It kind of started around then and has just stayed there, if that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Did your symptoms ever get noticeably worse for at least two weeks at any point in the past three years?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, there have definitely been stretches where I felt more worn down, but I can’t honestly point to a two-week period where it clearly got noticeably worse. It’s been more consistent than that, if that’s okay to say.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a two-week period or longer where you felt significantly worse than you do now?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had rough patches, but not where I’d say for two weeks or more I was significantly worse than I am now. It’s more been… kind of steady, I guess. I hope that’s the kind of answer you’re looking for.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever experience a period where your symptoms seemed to worsen gradually over time, rather than staying steady?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really gradually worsening, no. It’s been going on for about three years and three months, since I was moved into that higher-responsibility role mentoring adjusters and helping oversee the new workflow systems. Since then it’s felt more like it settled in and stayed there, rather than building up over time. If that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I’ve had rough patches, but not where I’d say for two weeks or more I was significantly worse than I am now. It’s more been… kind of steady, I guess. I hope that’s the kind of answer you’re looking for.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever experience a period where your symptoms seemed to worsen gradually over time, rather than staying steady?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really gradually worsening, no. It’s been going on for about three years and three months, since I was moved into that higher-responsibility role mentoring adjusters and helping oversee the new workflow systems. Since then it’s felt more like it settled in and stayed there, rather than building up over time. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had two weeks or more in the past three years where you felt so down or uninterested in things that you couldn't function like you usually do?\n\nPatient: I mean… not completely couldn’t function, I don’t think. I still show up and do what I’m supposed to, mostly. But yes, there have been stretches where it’s affected me. At work I’ve been missing details in audits and taking longer to sign off on files, which is embarrassing to admit. And outside of work, I’ve kind of pulled back from painting, hiking, volunteering… I’m mostly just trying to get through the week. Does that answer it?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.1, F33.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess I’ve just been really tired lately… I mean, maybe everyone is, but by midmorning at work, even just reviewing claim files or walking a junior adjuster through a claim, I feel like my body and my brain are already running on empty. I thought it might be worth coming in, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this tired?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a while, actually. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but probably about three years or so—maybe three years and a few months. It started around when I was moved into that higher-responsibility role, mentoring adjusters and helping oversee the new workflow systems. I kept thinking I’d adjust to it, you know?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I can still do things and be there with my family, but most days it’s like there’s this gray film over everything. Even when my husband is being kind, or we’re cooking together with the family, I can go through the motions… I just don’t really feel the enjoyment the way I used to, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have, somewhat. I don’t mean I never enjoy anything at all, but most days it’s like there’s a gray film over everything. Even when my husband is being sweet, or we’re cooking with the family, I can do it and smile and all that… I just don’t really feel it the way I used to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe… I mean, I still like being with my husband and cooking with the family. I don’t want to say there’s no pleasure at all. It’s just most days it feels kind of muted, like that gray film I mentioned. I can be there and go through the motions, but I’m not really feeling the enjoyment the way I used to… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d call it anxious, exactly. I mean, I worry about normal things, like work or family stuff, but I haven’t really noticed it being the main thing lately. It’s more just… I’m not sure. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your symptoms were significantly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, it’s kind of been fairly steady, I guess. Some days are definitely heavier than others, but I can’t think of a clear period where it was dramatically worse. Maybe I’m not remembering it the right way, but nothing really stands out like that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are your symptoms usually present for most of the day, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: It’s… usually there most of the day, I think. I can still do things, like be with my husband or cook with the family, so it’s not like I’m completely shut down. But that gray feeling is kind of hanging over things most days, and even when something should feel nice, I’m more just going through the motions. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the gray film over everything and not really enjoying things?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s been a while. Maybe a little over three years now—about three years and a few months. It seemed to start around when I got moved into that higher-responsibility role at work, mentoring adjusters and helping oversee the new workflow systems. I don’t want to make it sound like I couldn’t handle it, because I did handle it, but that’s about when this gray feeling started hanging around. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just a few weeks, where your symptoms were noticeably worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I could clearly point to. There are weeks that feel harder, sure, but I don’t know that I’d say they were noticeably worse than now for a few weeks straight. It’s more like it stays at this same dull level most of the time, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms ever intensified to the point where you felt significantly worse than you do currently?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ve had some rougher days here and there, but I don’t think it’s ever gotten dramatically worse than it is now. It’s more like it’s been steady, if that makes sense. I hope I’m answering that the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms ever been worse at some point in the past than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure I can say it was worse before. It’s been pretty steady, I think. Some days or weeks feel heavier than others, but not like a clearly worse period that stands out. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time when your symptoms seemed to worsen all at once?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not all at once, anyway. It’s more like it just kind of… sits there, if that makes sense. I’ve had days that feel harder, but I can’t think of a time where it suddenly got much worse. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms changed in intensity over the past three years?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been mostly the same level, not really big swings. It’s been going on for about three years… maybe three years and a few months now, since I got moved into that higher-responsibility role where I was mentoring adjusters and helping oversee the new workflow systems. It kind of started around then and has just stayed there, if that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Did your symptoms ever get noticeably worse for at least two weeks at any point in the past three years?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, there have definitely been stretches where I felt more worn down, but I can’t honestly point to a two-week period where it clearly got noticeably worse. It’s been more consistent than that, if that’s okay to say.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a two-week period or longer where you felt significantly worse than you do now?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had rough patches, but not where I’d say for two weeks or more I was significantly worse than I am now. It’s more been… kind of steady, I guess. I hope that’s the kind of answer you’re looking for.\n\nDoctor: Did you ever experience a period where your symptoms seemed to worsen gradually over time, rather than staying steady?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really gradually worsening, no. It’s been going on for about three years and three months, since I was moved into that higher-responsibility role mentoring adjusters and helping oversee the new workflow systems. Since then it’s felt more like it settled in and stayed there, rather than building up over time. If that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had two weeks or more in the past three years where you felt so down or uninterested in things that you couldn't function like you usually do?\n\nPatient: I mean… not completely couldn’t function, I don’t think. I still show up and do what I’m supposed to, mostly. But yes, there have been stretches where it’s affected me. At work I’ve been missing details in audits and taking longer to sign off on files, which is embarrassing to admit. And outside of work, I’ve kind of pulled back from painting, hiking, volunteering… I’m mostly just trying to get through the week. Does that answer it?\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a clear point when your symptoms became worse than they were before?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think the clearest point was when I got moved into that higher-responsibility role, mentoring the other adjusters and helping oversee the new workflow systems. That was about three years and three months ago. It wasn’t dramatic overnight, but that’s when things felt different from before, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I kept thinking I’d adjust to it, you know?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I can still do things and be there with my family, but most days it’s like there’s this gray film over everything. Even when my husband is being kind, or we’re cooking together with the family, I can go through the motions… I just don’t really feel the enjoyment the way I used to, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have, somewhat. I don’t mean I never enjoy anything at all, but most days it’s like there’s a gray film over everything. Even when my husband is being sweet, or we’re cooking with the family, I can do it and smile and all that… I just don’t really feel it the way I used to. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe… I mean, I still like being with my husband and cooking with the family. I don’t want to say there’s no pleasure at all. It’s just most days it feels kind of muted, like that gray film I mentioned. I can be there and go through the motions, but I’m not really feeling the enjoyment the way I used to… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried a lot lately?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t know if I’d call it anxious, exactly. I mean, I worry about normal things, like work or family stuff, but I haven’t really noticed it being the main thing lately. It’s more just… I’m not sure. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods where your symptoms were significantly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, it’s kind of been fairly steady, I guess. Some days are definitely heavier than others, but I can’t think of a clear period where it was dramatically worse. Maybe I’m not remembering it the right way, but nothing really stands out like that. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are your symptoms usually present for most of the day, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: It’s… usually there most of the day, I think. I can still do things, like be with my husband or cook with the family, so it’s not like I’m completely shut down. But that gray feeling is kind of hanging over things most days, and even when something should feel nice, I’m more just going through the motions. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the gray film over everything and not really enjoying things?\n\nPatient: I guess… it’s been a while. Maybe a little over three years now—about three years and a few months. It seemed to start around when I got moved into that higher-responsibility role at work, mentoring adjusters and helping oversee the new workflow systems. I don’t want to make it sound like I couldn’t handle it, because I did handle it, but that’s about when this gray feeling started hanging around. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just a few weeks, where your symptoms were noticeably worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way I could clearly point to. There are weeks that feel harder, sure, but I don’t know that I’d say they were noticeably worse than now for a few weeks straight. It’s more like it stays at this same dull level most of the time, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms ever intensified to the point where you felt significantly worse than you do currently?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I’ve had some rougher days here and there, but I don’t think it’s ever gotten dramatically worse than it is now. It’s more like it’s been steady, if that makes sense. I hope I’m answering that the right way.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms ever been worse at some point in the past than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure I can say it was worse before. It’s been pretty steady, I think. Some days or weeks feel heavier than others, but not like a clearly worse period that stands out. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a time when your symptoms seemed to worsen all at once?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Not all at once, anyway. It’s more like it just kind of… sits there, if that makes sense. I’ve had days that feel harder, but I can’t think of a time where it suddenly got much worse. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms changed in intensity over the past three years?\n\nPatient: I think it’s been mostly the same level, not really big swings. It’s been going on for about three years… maybe three years and a few months now, since I got moved into that higher-responsibility role where I was mentoring adjusters and helping oversee the new workflow systems. It kind of started around then and has just stayed there, if that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Did your symptoms ever get noticeably worse for at least two weeks at any point in the past three years?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, there have definitely been stretches where I felt more worn down, but I can’t honestly point to a two-week period where it clearly got noticeably worse. It’s been more consistent than that, if that’s okay to say.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a two-week period or longer where you felt significantly worse than you do now?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had rough patches, but not where I’d say for two weeks or more I was significantly worse than I am now. It’s more been… kind of steady, I guess. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
